#6861. How to Watch Every Movie in the Marvel Cinematic Universe

It’s easy. You only need time. And a Disney+ subscription.

Suddenly, we’re excited about Marvel again. Hitting theaters on December 18 is Avengers: Doomsday, the long-awaited next Avengers sequel. You may have heard that Robert Downey Jr. is back, only now in the role of the villainous Doctor Doom instead of Iron Man. As the man himself said back at San Diego Comic-Con in July 2024: “New mask, same task.” Whatever that means. (Also, if you think you ever heard of something called Avengers: The Kang Dynasty, no, you didn’t.)

Anyway, it’s been seven years since we last saw a new Avengers movie. Back then, you might have Googled something like, “How to watch every Marvel movie.” I have a gut feeling that’s what brought you here now. As Doomsday awakens dormant feelings about superheroes and multiverses, you probably need a guide to help you revisit all the best Marvel films or catch up on the ones you’ve missed. Lucky for you, it is easy to catch up. You just need time and a Disney+ subscription. If you start now, you’ll roll credits on The Fantastic Four: First Steps by December 17.

Here’s how to watch every Marvel movie before Avengers: Doomsday. Check back a year from now, when we’re doing it all over again for Avengers: Secret Wars.

Here’s the good news: Except for Spider-Man: Brand New Day, which came out this summer and is now on digital, every Marvel Cinematic Universe movie is available on Disney+. Every. Single. One.

Iron Man? Yes. Infinity War? Yup. Captain America: The Winter Soldier? Hell yeah. Guardians of the Galaxy? All three, baby! Ant-Man and the Wasp: Quantumania? Maybe skip that one. And since Disney acquired Fox in 2018, you’ll also find all the X-Men, Deadpool, and the two Fantastic Four movies from the mid-2000s.

There’s also plenty of Marvel television. Not just streaming shows like WandaVision, The Falcon and the Winter Soldier, Ms. Marvel, and the recent VisionQuest, but also network shows like Agents of S.H.I.E.L.D. and Agent Carter, as well as the Netflix Defenders-verse. If you’re really hardcore, you can also watch the handful of “One Shot” short films that were originally released on several Blu-rays.

The only “Marvel movies” you can’t watch on Disney+ are (most) of the Sony-produced movies. These include Morbius, Madame Web, Kraven the Hunter, and the Venom trilogy. But because distribution rights are funny, the Tobey Maguire and Andrew Garfield Spider-Man movies are on Disney+, along with Spider-Man: Across the Spider-Verse as I write this.

I hear you. Short of time-traveling back to 2000 and watching every movie as they come out, it feels impossible to “catch up” with all of it now. Especially since Avengers: Doomsday is once again dealing with the multiverse, which means even “non-canon” movies (like, say, the Blade or Punisher movies) might get a shout-out.

Luckily, Marvel knows your pain. On Disney+, a new “Countdown to Avengers: Doomsday” sub-page highlights the most important movies. While there are glaring omissions, watching only the highlighted movies can bump just about anyone up to speed. Even people who say they’ve “never seen one of these things.”

For your convenience, I’ve listed below EVERY movie from the Fox X-Men universe, the Sony Spider-Man universes, and the Marvel Cinematic Universe, in order of theatrical release. This is the only correct “viewing order.” Do not listen to YouTubers who insist on some “timeline order” nonsense. You can be an adult and watch these things exactly how they were produced and released. The movies Marvel deems essential for Doomsday prep are listed in bold, with the trailers attached.

Blade is rightfully credited with kickstarting the 21st-century superhero boom, but the X-Men films set the tone as must-see summer blockbusters. The first film in Fox’s X-Men series introduced audiences to mutants, with Hugh Jackman as Wolverine, the late Patrick Stewart as Charles Xavier, and Ian McKellen as radical extremist Magneto, who leads his Brotherhood in a fight for equal rights. Famke Janssen and James Marsden, who also starred as Jean Grey and Cyclops, respectively, will return in Doomsday, but Halle Berry’s Storm is sitting it out.

The battle for mutant liberation continues when Colonel Stryker (Brian Cox) kidnaps Xavier, forcing the X-Men to align with the Brotherhood of Mutants. In this better-than-you-remember sequel, mutantkind stands side by side for the sake of their survival. Though X2 predates shared universes, its scope, scale, and political intrigue make it one of the greatest and most underrated sequels of all time.

“I am Iron Man.” The resurrection of Robert Downey Jr.’s career blasted off to the stratosphere, along with the Marvel Cinematic Universe itself, in a movie that officially kicked off the MCU. Kidnapped by terrorists and forced into a cave with scraps, playboy futurist Tony Stark (Downey Jr.) escapes as the armored superhero Iron Man and returns home to find that his enemies are far closer to him than he suspects. When the smoke clears, a shadowy figure named Nick Fury (Samuel L. Jackson) informs him that the universe is way bigger than he knows.

Once, Chris Evans was the Human Torch. Suddenly, he wasn’t. As the Marvel Cinematic Universe picked up steam, the sweltering summer of 2011 saw Captain America: The First Avenger. Set in World War II, the first Marvel “prequel” stars Evans as the brave shrimp of Brooklyn, Steve Rogers, who becomes the first to endure the experimental “Super Soldier” program. As Captain America, Rogers takes the fight to Red Skull, whose dark ambition lies beyond the Third Reich.

There once was an idea: To bring together a remarkable group of cinematic superheroes to see if they can make cinematic history. In 2012, Marvel did just that. The first crossover Avengers movie saw the growing roster of Marvel’s cinematic superheroes team up for the very first time. Though Marvel movies have only gotten bigger and even better since, we cannot deny the seismic impact of The Avengers.

Captain America: The Winter Soldier (2014)

After The Avengers planted the seeds for Thanos, all of Marvel’s storytelling shifted to an adaptation of the 1991 comic crossover The Infinity Gauntlet. In 2018, Marvel released Avengers: Infinity War, the third Avengers movie that forever changed the MCU’s status quo. With Josh Brolin as Thanos, the Marvel Universe’s many heroes band together to stave off the greatest threat anyone had ever seen. While audiences knew another movie was around the corner, its shock ending dared to ask the unthinkable: What happens when heroes lose?

It all came down to this. In 2019, on the same weekend Game of Thrones staged its own final battle, Avengers: Endgame changed the trajectory of the Marvel Universe once more. The fourth Avengers film sees the heroes dust off for a rematch against Thanos. But before they can do that, they must travel back in time through their own history to collect the Infinity Stones if they want to stand a chance.

In September 2026, in preparation for Doomsday, Marvel released an extended cut of Avengers: Endgame, titled Encore. It comes with a few extra minutes of scenes that frankly ruin the grace of Endgame in its original form, but if you really want to see clearer connective tissue to Doomsday, check out Encore.

In the 1970s, one of the most popular Marvel Comics titles wasn’t The Amazing Spider-Man, Uncanny X-Men, or The Avengers. It was Master of Kung Fu, starring an ass-kicker named Shang-Chi. He was a master martial artist and the son of a supervillain (Fu Manchu in the comics) who, over time, works as a secret agent for MI6. He was created by Steve Englehart and Jim Starlin (who also created Thanos) in the spirit of Bruce Lee and the TV show Kung Fu.

After decades of obscurity, the box-office success of Crazy Rich Asians in 2018 inspired Marvel to dig into the longboxes and put the spotlight back on Shang-Chi. In 2021, Simu Liu brought him to life in Shang-Chi and the Legend of the Ten Rings, an origin movie that introduces Shang-Chi for the 21st century. Hong Kong screen legend Tony Leung (In the Mood for Love) costars as Wenwu, Shang-Chi’s father, who runs the terrorist group the Ten Rings—named after Wenwu’s mysterious alien weapons—which kidnapped Tony Stark in the first Iron Man.

Shang-Chi’s use of the Ten Rings has yet-unknown ties to the wider Marvel Universe. After five years, we may finally have some answers in Avengers: Doomsday.

After Spider-Man was publicly unmasked as Peter Parker at the end of Spider-Man: Far From Home, the teenage superhero turns to Sorcerer Supreme Doctor Strange (Benedict Cumberbatch) for help. But when the magic goes wrong, the multiverse cracks open, and villains from other realities—as in, other Spider-Man franchises—seep through the cracks. Tom Holland’s Spider-Man teams up with two veteran Spideys, played by Tobey Maguire and Andrew Garfield (remember them?), to put the universe back in one piece.

Doctor Strange saved the universe in Avengers: Endgame and restored the multiverse in Spider-Man: No Way Home. But can he save himself? In this direct sequel to 2016’s Doctor Strange and set after both Spider-Man: No Way Home and the Disney+ series WandaVision, Stephen must help protect a young girl, America Chavez (Xochitl Gomez), from a vengeful Wanda (Elizabeth Olsen), who wants to use America’s unique powers to reunite with her own lost family.

Originally billed as Marvel’s first horror movie (its very title alluding to Lovecraft’s At the Mountains of Madness), creative reshuffling led to Evil Dead and Spider-Man director Sam Raimi helming the movie. This explains the movie’s uniquely darker tone than one might expect from a typical Marvel tentpole. Cameos in this film tie directly to some of Marvel’s other far corners, like TV shows What If...? and Inhumans, as well as the Fox X-Men movies.

Werewolf by Night (2022), a “Marvel Studios Special Presentation” on Disney+

The real-life passing of Chadwick Boseman in 2020 left a huge hole in everyone’s hearts—and Marvel in a difficult place. Without its star, writer/director Ryan Coogler redesigned his plans for the sequel to his Earth-shattering 2018 hit.

Taking inspiration from Reginald Hudlin’s run in the comics, Black Panther: Wakanda Forever sets the stage for a new Black Panther: Shuri, T’Challa’s genius sister, played by Letitia Wright. But before Shuri can take up her brother’s mantle, she must first learn how heavy crowns are when another kingdom emerges from the depths and threatens Wakanda. The sequel introduces Namor (Tenoch Huerta), from the underwater world Talokan (originally Atlantis in the comics). The two will play a larger role in Avengers: Doomsday.

The Guardians of the Galaxy Holiday Special (2022), a “Marvel Studios Special Presentation” on Disney+

After Hugh Jackman retired from playing Wolverine in the 2017 masterpiece Logan, Marvel and Ryan Reynolds shelled out a truckload of money to get him back and into some yellow spandex at last. The third movie in the Deadpool series, Deadpool & Wolverine, marked the MCU debut of Ryan Reynolds’ scrotum-faced Deadpool, whose dream of joining the Avengers clashes with the collapse of his own reality. More cameos, callbacks, and cusses fill the movie’s two-hour runtime.

While Jackman and Channing Tatum—who was once tapped to play the X-Men member Gambit, which Deadpool & Wolverine finally made a reality—will return in Doomsday, it’s unknown whether Reynolds will too. But if his surprise appearance at Comic-Con means anything, then...

When Steve Rogers gave Sam Wilson (Anthony Mackie) the shield of Captain America in Endgame, Sam wasn’t ready to take up the responsibility so soon. He still had his own things to work out. After the Disney+ series The Falcon and the Winter Soldier, Sam now flies the skies as Captain America, with the 2025 film Captain America: Brave New World marking the start of his era as the Star-Spangled Man. In addition to being the fourth Captain America movie, the movie revisits the events of 2008’s The Incredible Hulk and 2021’s Eternals.

The Marvel franchise was built on a studio elevating comic book benchwarmers into A-listers. That tradition was upheld in the 2025 movie Thunderbolts*. Based on a Marvel Comics series where supervillains form a team, the big-screen version recruits several “forgotten” antiheroes from different corners of the MCU. Together, they form a new team that must stave off their own assassination and save the world—on their terms. You can watch the movie cold, but know that many of these characters come from movies like Captain America: The Winter Soldier, Black Widow, Ant-Man and the Wasp, and the TV show The Falcon and the Winter Soldier.

And now, we step into a whole ‘nother world. After multiple attempts at a big-screen Fantastic Four, the Marvel Cinematic Universe launched its own take on the First Family in The Fantastic Four: First Steps. Set on a different Earth in 1964, the Fantastic Four rise to the occasion when the planet is threatened by Galactus.

Look. Don’t put yourself through that. There are too many. If you insist on watching any of them, just watch Loki. It has two seasons and just 12 episodes total, so you can knock it out in one weekend. Loki takes place after Avengers: Endgame (technically after just The Avengers, but how and why is too long to explain), and of all the shows, it bears the strongest ties to Doomsday. Talk about a glorious purpose.

#6858. The Cutest Way to Wear Your Short Hair Right Now? A Flipped Bob.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

What’s one thing Ariana Grande and Hailey Bieber have in common right now? Their flipped bobs. (Bobiana Grande, anyone?) Bobs in all forms have basically taken over the hair world, from French bobs and Italian bobs to blunt bobs and bell-bottom bobs. I would’ve guessed, at this point, that bobs had bobbed their last bob. Alas, another one has appeared: the flipped bob. (At this point, there are probably almost as many types of bobs as times I’ve said “bob” in this paragraph. Anyway.)

Inspired by the voluminous, flipped-out hairstyles of the 1960s, the fun, bouncy finish on this retro look is super chic. Plus, they’re so in for this time of year: “Flipped bobs are very trendy for the holiday season and a perfect style for holiday parties,” says celebrity hairstylist Nicolas Flores. And the best part? If you already have a bob, you don’t necessarily need a new haircut to get in on the trend. A blow-dryer-and-brush combo or flat iron can do the trick to give your existing bob that sought-after flick. Looking for some flipped bob ideas? Ahead, some expert tips from Flores on getting the look and plenty of inspo pics to reference before you start styling.

A flipped bob is just a classic bob haircut, but with one very important twist: The ends are styled outward rather than straight or curled in. It “focuses more on curling the ends outward and away from the face, giving a fun, flirty look,” says Flores. The result? A playful, totally-bouncy hairstyle that was made popular in the ‘60s by beauty icons like Jackie Kennedy and Mary Tyler Moore and is now being worn by some of today’s beauty icons like Grande and Bieber.

While the original flipped bob had a ton of volume and serious outward curl, the current versions we’re seeing are a little more subtle, sleek, and versatile, sometimes just barely flicked away from your face. The style works well on all lengths of bobs, from chin-grazing cuts to longer, shoulder-length lobs. You can also customize them with bangs, layers, a side part, or however you normally like to wear your bob.

Before any styling happens, think about your haircut. If you already have a bob, you don’t need to worry about cutting it shorter or anything like that. But if you’re getting a bob for the first time with the intention of styling it with the ends flipped out, “tell your stylist you don’t want to go too short,” says Flores, since the styling can make the hair look even shorter. “I always recommend an inch-and-a-half to two inches below the hairline to get that perfect length,” he says.

“I’d start with damp hair, a little volumizing mousse, and some heat protectant,” he says. The Cosmo Holy Grail Beauty Award-winning Nexxus Hy-Volume Weightless Lift Cloud Foam and Roz Air Thickening Spray are two great options. Section your hair and apply two to three pumps of each product at your roots, working it through to the ends.

“Using a medium round brush, blow dry the hair nice and smooth, giving it a little lift at the roots,” says Flores.

Don’t forget to focus on the star of the show: those flippy ends. “Once you get to the ends, turn the brush upward and away from the neck to create that cute little flip,” says Flores. “Give it a quick blast of cool air before releasing the brush so it holds its shape.” That’ll give a more natural-looking flip, but for something more dramatic, reach for a flat iron after you finish the blowout. “If you want the flip to be a little more defined, you can go back in with a flat iron and gently flick the ends outward,” adds Flores. “Just a little turn of the wrist is all you need!”

After that, you’re pretty much at the finish line: “Finish with a light hairspray to keep everything in place without making it stiff,” says Flores. “The goal is to keep the bob looking soft, bouncy, and effortless.” Spritz it lightly all over, but focus on the ends especially to keep that flippy look in place all day.

Now that you know how to style a flipped bob, check out these flipped bob ideas so you can recreate the fun look for yourself.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s written about all things hair, from cool hair color trends to the best haircuts worth trying.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

#6856. A Guide to the Best Canada Goose Jackets

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

High performance or high fashion? There’s a pick for each.

Since 1957, Canada Goose has been crushing the luxury outerwear game. And if its decades of history tell you one thing, it’s that Canada Goose isn’t going anywhere anytime soon. In fact, one of the best decisions that you can make is investing in a piece or two (if you haven’t already) because it’s a purchase that’s going to pay off. The high-quality designs are durable, practical, and stand at a timeless intersection where fashion meets function.

The biggest issue we have with Canada Goose is that there are too many options to choose from. You’re probably already aware that the brand has more than just its hefty parkas. Sure, it’s got those too, of course, but we also love its bombers, flannels, and fleeces. Canada Goose brand has even been venturing into the high-fashion space, with its creative director Haider Ackermann debuting collaborations with the NBA’s Shai Gilgeous-Alexander, CNCPTS, and KidSuper, among others.To help you find the right fit for you, we did a full breakdown for you. Below, find our official guide to the six best Canada Goose jackets. You’re not going to regret getting any of these.

This bomber jacket has some history to it. Originally designed over 25 years ago, this iteration of the jacket fuses modern functionality with classic bomber detailing, like a double placket, pen pocket, and ribbed waist. The archive-inspired satin gives it a glossy finish, but it’s still subtle enough that it will go with pretty much just about anything. Wear it with some black jeans, combat or Chelsea boots, and you’ve got a badass fit.

Every man should own a bomber jacket, so if for some reason the Seguin Aviator didn’t resonate with you, maybe this one will. Inspired by the brand’s 2004 Air Force parka, this bomber adds a warm fleece for insulation—as well as style points. With five pockets (two welt pockets at the chest, two lower pockets with coin-snaps, one zippered sleeve pocket), it masters practicality.

You can never go wrong with a traditional puffer. It’s actually more of a prerequisite than anything else, especially if you live somewhere with an actual winter. Canada Goose’s puffer is top-of-the-line, as it’s ultra-lightweight, super soft, and durable. So many puffer jackets weigh you down, but this one doesn’t. Its features are also endless: There are backpack straps, recessed rib-knit cuffs that add comfort and lock in heat, interior drawcord to customize fit, two exterior fleece-lined handwarmer pockets with zipper closures, three interior pockets, two mesh drop-in pockets, one security pocket—and more.

If you want a streamlined parka that doesn’t have too much going on on the outside—but still has a ton of cool features inside—the Langford is the one for you. It has a sleek silhouette that’s slightly longer than a puffer, and it’s even warmer than a puffer, too. With 625 Fill Power down inside, it can protect you in temperatures down to -13°F, and it will look great while doing it. It also has an adjustable down-filled hood, lined chin guard, two-way zipper underneath snap closures, and fleece-lined upper hand pockets so you can really keep the cold out and turn up the warmth.

The Expedition Parka is Canada Goose’s original—and most iconic—extreme-weather jacket. Initially developed for scientists working in Antarctica, this jacket is made to keep you warm in the most extreme of environments. It does that—and then some—with its elasticized snow skirt for added protection, interior drawcord at the waist to customize fit and retain warmth, and double-entry pockets for easy access. We could elaborate even more here, but you can just check out our love letter to the iconic parka for all the details.

But this version is even cooler than the original: It’s part of the Snow Goose line, which is basically creative director Haider Ackermann’s passion project. It features some more fashion-forward designs, stuff that’s harder to wear. However, this version of the Expedition Parka keeps the classic shape, but it comes in two seasonal colors, so you’ll stand out from everyone else wearing the same model, but won’t feel like you’re on the runway. It’s a win-win.

We know this puffer jacket looks like a sweater, but that’s kind of the point: It has the fun design of a winter sweater and the warmth of a down jacket—and when you add in the soft feel of the exterior from the wool-alpaca blend, you’re getting a lot of good stuff. It doesn’t look too bulky, but it can keep you warm down to -4°F, if you can believe it. It’s a good alternative to the more classic polyester or nylon puffer jackets; just be careful about taking it out in heavy rain or snow, and keep in mind that the exterior material requires more delicate handling than the usual puffer jacket material.

#6853. Are We Doomed to Get Cheated On?

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

At this point, being cheated on feels like an inevitable part of the modern dating experience. Every other week, it seems, another man with a wife, a public-facing career, and absolutely no sense of self-preservation is getting caught with a woman who is decidedly not his wife. And thanks to the internet, we all get front-row seats to the fallout.

If you’ve logged onto any form of social media, you were probably inundated with headlines about Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's infidelity. And while the details of his three—yes, three—affairs seem unique (one being with a literal spy), there’s an odd sense of deja vu in there. Remember Ethan Klein? Or the Coldplay kiss-cam scandal? I know we haven’t forgotten about Jay-Z. While I don’t know any of these people personally, it’s hard not to feel affected by these headlines. I feel as though every time I look at the news or scroll through TikTok, I find myself wondering if my own partner has a secret second phone.

As entertaining (and occasionally horrifying) as these scandals are, they've helped create a rather bleak narrative about modern relationships: Everybody cheats, men in particular, and trusting someone is basically asking to get your heart broken.

“When a cheating scandal goes viral, millions of people watch it very often for the shock and may presume everyone is doing it,” Seema Sharma, LMFT tells Cosmopolitan. “The numbers, however, don't back that fear up.”

So why does it feel like everyone is cheating?

Cheating isn't nearly as universal as the internet would have you believe.

According to a 2025 analysis of General Social Survey data by the Institute for Family Studies, 17 percent of ever-married Americans ages 25 to 54 reported having had sex outside their marriage in the 1990s, compared with 13 percent in 2021 and 2022.

Of course, there are things to consider here: Infidelity isn’t just limited to marriage, and of course, this all depends on what people are willing to share. Because the survey focuses on sexual infidelity within marriage, it doesn't capture every situationship betrayal, secret DM, or emotional affair happening in every kind of relationship.

A 2025 YouGov survey found that roughly one-quarter of American adults admitted to having engaged in sexual activities with someone other than their main romantic partner without that partner's consent.

But just because cheating happens—which we were well aware of already—doesn’t mean it's practically guaranteed when you get into a relationship.

“What I often see is the cost of the fear itself,” Sharma says. “People go into dating braced for betrayal. They check phones, they hold back, and sometimes they leave first so they can't be left.”

And this collective obsession with catching cheaters might be making it harder to actually enjoy the relationships we're in.

I don’t like to point fingers... but it’s hard not to when it comes to cheating. This is because men have traditionally reported higher rates of extramarital sex than women. But the gap is getting smaller.

According to the Institute for Family Studies analysis, approximately 21 percent of men who were ever married (ages 25 to 54) reported having cheated in the 1990s, compared with just 11 percent in 2021 and 2022. Meanwhile, women's reported infidelity rates remained relatively stable, hovering around 14 percent in the most recent period. Again, this only paints the picture for married couples.

While men and women may sometimes describe their reasons for cheating differently, the motivations aren't necessarily as gendered as we'd like to believe.

"Underneath, I hear the same things from both,” Sharma says. “They wanted to feel wanted, or seen, or authentic in their relationship, but didn't.”

Speaking as someone who has been cheated on, I know firsthand how easy it is to start wondering what you did to push your partner to betray you. But it’s important to remember that cheating isn’t always a symptom of a miserable relationship. Research shows that 56 percent of men and 34 percent of women who had affairs described their marriages as happy.

"Often an affair is more about the person and not the relationship,” Sharma explains. “Someone loves their partner and still goes looking for a version of themselves they feel they lost.”

Of course, understanding someone's motivation for cheating isn't the same thing as excusing it. Feeling unfulfilled, insecure, or disconnected might explain a person's behavior, but it doesn't make betraying their partner inevitable.

Given that Gen Z has practically turned relationship surveillance into a competitive sport—checking Instagram follows, analyzing likes, investigating suspicious Venmo transactions—you might assume younger people are particularly prone to infidelity.

But just 12 percent of Americans ages 18 to 29 reported ever engaging in sexual activities with someone else without their partner's consent, compared with 29 percent of adults ages 45 to 64. Of course, older people have had more years and relationships in which to potentially cheat, so that doesn't necessarily mean Gen Z is inherently more faithful. But it does seem that younger generations are changing how they think about infidelity.

"For older generations, cheating mostly meant sex. Younger people are dealing with DMs, likes, dating apps that never got deleted, and exes who are one click away," Sharma says.

While younger people might have more opportunities to engage in what we've collectively decided to call “microcheating,” they're also navigating a dating landscape where the boundaries of monogamy aren't always so obvious.

Is liking your ex's thirst trap cheating? What about flirting with someone in your DMs? Or maintaining a suspiciously close friendship with someone you once hooked up with? The answer, increasingly, depends on whom you ask.

A 2024 analysis published by the Institute for Family Studies, found that 76 percent of Americans considered a secret, in-person emotional relationship cheating, while 72 percent said the same about a secret online emotional relationship.

The upside, according to Sharma, is that younger couples may be more willing to discuss these boundaries rather than assuming they're on the same page.

"Younger couples are much more willing to sit down and define what counts," she says—which to me personally, feels like a step in the right direction despite all of the bad press relationships have been getting.

In a perfect world, our partner would never betray us. In a semi-perfect world, we would at least easily detach or leave the second that they do. But as we all know, we live in neither.

Sure, statistically speaking, infidelity isn't exactly great for a relationship's longevity. The 2025 Institute for Family Studies analysis found that 52 percent of ever-married adults ages 25 to 54 who reported having cheated were currently divorced or separated, compared with 15 percent of those who hadn't cheated. (Of course, this data doesn’t establish that infidelity directly caused those divorces.)

But for many couples, cheating doesn't always mean the end. A 2014 study published in Couple and Family Psychology: Research and Practice followed couples who had undergone relationship therapy. Five years later, 43 percent of couples whose infidelity had been revealed were divorced, compared with 80 percent of couples whose infidelity remained secret.

Those numbers sound dramatic, but the study included only 19 couples who experienced infidelity, so we can't assume the same percentages apply to all relationships. Still, the researchers found that couples who remained married after infidelity reported levels of relationship satisfaction and stability comparable to couples who hadn't experienced an affair.

Now, this isn’t me condoning you staying with a cheater. But we each have to determine for ourselves what we’re willing to work through and forgive.

Sharma says that three things matter most for couples who are able to move past infidelity: "The affair has to be over. The person who cheated has to be willing to answer hard questions for a long time without getting defensive. And both people have to want to understand how they got there."

That doesn't mean rebuilding trust is easy—or that anyone is obligated to forgive a partner who has betrayed them.

"Recovery is slow. I tell couples to expect a couple of years, not a couple of months," Sharma adds.

And while it's comforting to know that some relationships can recover, it's equally important to recognize that leaving after infidelity is a completely valid choice. Nobody owes someone a second chance simply because reconciliation is possible.

Short answer? No. Longer answer? Unfortunately, there's no magical way to guarantee that someone will never betray your trust. But that doesn't mean you should spend your entire relationship preparing for the worst.

For starters, Sharma cautions against becoming obsessed with identifying potential cheaters. Sure, sudden secrecy, emotional withdrawal, or defensiveness might warrant a conversation, but none of those behaviors automatically means your partner is being unfaithful.

"The signs that hold up are changes, not single behaviors,” she explains. “Any of those can also mean stress, depression, or trouble at work.”

And if you've reached the point of regularly checking your partner's phone, tracking their location, or conducting a full FBI investigation every time they follow someone attractive on Instagram, the relationship might already have a trust problem worth addressing.

“If you find yourself playing detective, that tells you something about the relationship no matter what you find,” Sharma says. “A direct conversation will get you further than a search through their messages.”

Ultimately, the research paints a much less apocalyptic picture of modern relationships than the internet does. Cheating is real, it can be devastating, and it's common enough that we shouldn't dismiss people's concerns. But it isn't inevitable, despite how much our algorithm may have us believe otherwise. While the headlines might make it seem like monogamy is on its deathbed, there's little evidence that we're suddenly living through an unprecedented cheating epidemic.

“Faithfulness is less about finding the one perfect person who would never do it and more about what two people build,” Sharma says.

This means communicating about boundaries, addressing resentment before it festers, and choosing partners who demonstrate honesty and accountability.

“If you walk into every relationship expecting betrayal, you'll protect yourself so well that no one can get close,” Sharma says. “That's a lonely way to live, and it can be worked on.”

So no, we're not all doomed to get cheated on. We might, however, be doomed to hear about every celebrity affair, influencer breakup, and suspicious Instagram like for the rest of eternity. And frankly, that's exhausting enough.

#6846. The “Scottish Country House” Look Is the Cozy Interior Trend Designers Say to Know This Season

You don't need a Highland cottage to nail this heritage-inspired look.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

You don't need a sprawling stone cottage in the Highlands, a sweeping countryside view, or a roaring fireplace to bring a little Scottish country-house charm into your home. In fact, the appeal of this cozy, heritage-inspired aesthetic is that it can work just as beautifully in a city apartment as it does in a centuries-old estate.

The secret is mixing those old-world details with pieces that feel fresh and livable, so your home doesn't end up looking like a period drama set. A tartan runner on the dining table, a cashmere throw draped over your favorite reading chair, or a few leather accents on the coffee table can bring just the right amount of countryside character to an otherwise modern space. Strathberry's new tartan homeware collection is another lovely entry point, giving the traditional pattern a contemporary spin that feels right at home in all kinds of interiors.

Whether you're fully embracing your countryside daydreams or just looking to make your space feel a little warmer and more layered this winter, there are plenty of ways to bring the look home. Read on for the Scottish country-house-inspired touches that make this aesthetic so charming—and how to work them into your own space.

If there's one pattern that deserves a starring role in a Scottish country-inspired home, it's tartan. And you don't have to limit it to a throw blanket or a few accent pillows. Designer Kaitlin Smith recommends “using plaid for a wallpaper or even as an accent pillow” to bring that distinctly Scottish feel into a space.

For a bolder take, try a tartan wallpaper in a powder room, entryway, or tucked-away reading nook. If you'd rather ease into the look, a single plaid pillow or upholstered accent piece can make just as much of a statement. With so many different tartan patterns and colorways to choose from, you can find one that brings a little heritage-inspired character to your home without making the whole room feel like a theme.

A Scottish country house should feel like it's been lived in for generations, not styled within an inch of its life. That's where woven baskets come in.

Noel Gatts, a designer and HGTV personality, recommends introducing “some slouchy woven baskets” throughout the home, whether at the entryway for shoes and umbrellas or in a living room or bedroom to hold extra blankets. Their slightly relaxed shape and natural texture add to that collected, lived-in feeling while giving everyday clutter somewhere to go.

Try a larger basket beside the front door, a smaller one tucked under a console or an oversized version next to your favorite reading chair. It's a practical detail that also brings in the natural materials that make this aesthetic feel so grounded. Plus, having a cozy throw within arm's reach is never a bad idea.

You can practically picture a pot of tea warming by the fire in a Scottish country house, and Gatts recommends leaning into that feeling with a thoughtful arrangement of crockery and candles.

“Feature a fireplace or dining table with tea pots, earthenware, and an installation of candles and/or candlesticks,” she suggests. This is a lovely opportunity to bring out those pieces you might otherwise save for special occasions, from vintage teapots and handmade ceramic bowls to mismatched candlesticks and well-loved serving dishes. Grouping them together creates a charming focal point without making the space feel overly formal.

Candlelight is especially important here, adding a soft, welcoming glow that feels worlds away from bright overhead lighting. And if you don't have a fireplace, a dining table, mantel or console can give you the same cozy effect.

A Scottish country house shouldn't feel too coordinated, so don't be afraid to bring a few different patterns into the mix. As designer Sarah Cole suggests, “try pairing a wool plaid with a botanical print, a small geometric pattern or a traditional rug.”

The combination adds visual interest and gives a room that layered, lived-in quality that makes country-house interiors so inviting. You can keep the look cohesive by pulling a few warm, autumnal colors throughout the patterns—like russets, burgundies, forest greens and ochres—while varying their scale so they don't compete with one another. It's a simple way to make the space feel more personal and collected over time.

The Scottish country-house look has a wonderful appreciation for pieces that feel like they've been around awhile. Rather than filling every surface with brand-new accessories, Gatts suggests bringing in “found objects with a sense of history” made from weathered wood, pottery, or patinated brass. These are the kinds of details that give a room its personality, whether it's an old wooden box on a coffee table, a vintage ceramic vessel or a brass object with a little wear and patina.

Don't worry about everything matching, either. A mix of materials and finishes makes the space feel more authentic and less like you ordered the entire room in one shopping trip. Gatts recommends using these pieces on a coffee table for “the perfect antique flair,” but a bookshelf, sideboard, or entryway console would work just as well.

Finally, a few well-chosen antique details can give a room that Highland lodge feeling without requiring a full redesign. Jill Wheeler of Birch Hill Interiors recommends adding “a touch of polished brass like antique candlesticks or wall sconces to give the space a refined look.” Try styling a pair of vintage brass candlesticks on a mantel, placing one on a coffee table, or swapping in brass wall sconces beside a favorite reading chair.

Wheeler also suggests incorporating antiques on a mantel, coffee table, or built-in, noting that “antiques can help bring the outside in and tell a story.” The mix of aged finishes and meaningful finds adds a sense of history and character that makes a Scottish country-inspired space feel genuinely lived in.

#6845. Warren Beatty and Annette Bening’s Former L.A. Mansion Lists for Nearly $15 Million

The Hollywood power couple custom-built the gated Beverly Hills house.

The longtime Beverly Hills home of Warren Beatty and Annette Bening will soon hit the market, ELLE Decor can exclusively reveal. The married couple custom-built the gated residence in 1992 and raised their four kids there. The stars are one of Hollywood’s most famous power couples: Beatty has received 14 Academy Award nominations, winning for Best Director in 1982, and three Golden Globe Awards, while Bening has received five Oscar nominations, including Best Actress for 1999’s American Beauty, for which she won the Golden Globe.

The A-list actors parted with their longtime property in 2012, selling it for $6.7 million, per Zillow. Its new asking price will be $14.995 million.

The nine-bedroom estate’s current owners—hotelier Jonathan Baker and his wife, Jenny—acquired the property directly from Beatty and Bening, then spearheaded a $5 million renovation that concluded in 2019. They modified the Mediterranean-style home to have a more contemporary feel, with minimal moldings, glossy surfaces, modern furnishings, and bold splashes of color.

However, they still paid homage to the estate’s movie-star pedigree, creating what they call the “Warren Beatty Theater,” a 17-seat screening room in what was once Beatty’s home office.

Other nods to the legendary actor—who starred in classics like Bonnie and Clyde, Dick Tracy, and Bulworth—included integrating his drop-down projection screen into the new living room. Additional perks include a wine room with a suspended-wire storage system, a wood-paneled dressing room with plenty of built-ins, and a network of terraces.

The home’s amenities continue on the manicured grounds, which feature an infinity-edge pool, a lighted tennis court, multiple al fresco entertaining areas, and a separate guest house.

The couple are parents to four children, among them Ella Beatty, who is following in her parents’ acting footsteps and recently played Lizzie Borden in the Netflix series Monster: The Lizzie Borden Story.

The Beverly Hills house will be listed for $14.995 million with Cory Weiss of Douglas Elliman and Jacob Greene of the Altman Brothers at Douglas Elliman.

Thoughtful, must-have devices for travelers, wellness buffs, the dude who never wants to leave his house, and more.

#6841. Meet the ‘Guarding Stars’ Cast and Characters

The ‘Gossip Girl’ and ‘Gilmore Girls’ crossover we deserve.

Netflix is stepping up its Christmas romance game with a book adaptation that’s action-packed and super sweet. It also, not to brag, has a pretty iconic millennial pairing and more in the cast. Let this be your guide to the stars of Guarding Stars.

The film is based on the 2022 novel The Bodyguard by Katherine Center, not to be confused with the 1992 romantic thriller The Bodyguard starring Kevin Costner and Whitney Houston or the Netflix drama series Bodyguard starring Richard Madden. This is strictly a home-for-the-holidays romantic comedy about a pretend relationship that a superstar, played by Jared Padalecki, and his bodyguard, played by Leighton Meester, fake so that her family doesn’t know she’s in danger.

Most of the characters are from the book, but there are a few newcomers and changes to keep readers on their toes. There’s also a character called “Brody” played by Jake Foy (Designated Survivor) we know very little about. And a lot of the supporting cast is played by folks who made their names in the sports and/or music industry. Get to know them and who they play in Guarding Stars below.

Hanna is an EPA, executive protection agent, assigned to Jack.

Leighton Meester is best known for playing Blair Waldorf on Gossip Girl, and recently recurred on I Love LA and The Buccaneers. Other notable TV roles include How I Met Your Father, Single Parents, and Making History. Meester’s filmography also includes The Roommates, Life Partners, Country Strong, Monte Carlo, and Going the Distance. Real ones also know she released an album in 2014.

Jack is an A-List actor with an escalating stalker problem.

There are two types of people in this world: those know how Jared Padalecki as Dean from Gilmore Girls and those who know him as Sam from Supernatural. Maybe there’s even a third kind of person who only knows him from Walker. Still, those are three pretty major credits. Movie-wise, you’ve also seen him in House of Wax, Flight of the Phoenix, Friday the 13h (2009), and Christmas Cottage.

Andie MacDowell won an Independent Spirit Award for Sex, Lies, and Videotape and is a rom-com queen best known for Groundhog Day and Four Weddings and a Funeral. She also appeared in Monte Carlo, and more recently in films like Magic Mike XXL, The Other Zoey, and Ready or Not as well as the series Cedar Cove and the miniseries Maid.

Noah LaLonde is a former hockey player and actor who plays Cole Walter on My Life with the Walter Boys. His film credits include Asbury Park and Deer Camp ‘86.

Siobhan Williams’ first job was playing Jamie on Heartland. Her other television credits include UnREAL, Black Box, Deadly Class, Sacred Lies, The Hunting Party, and the upcoming season of Mayfair Witches. She also had a supporting role in the 2018 film Welcome to Marwen.

Walker Hayes is a country singer who has released five studio albums. The song “Fancy Like” won a Billboard Music Award in 2022. He has also been nominated at the Grammy Awards, CMT Awards, and AMAs.

Taylor is one of Hanna’s former EPA colleagues and former best friend.

Rachael Ancheril is a Canadian actress you know from Rookie Blue, Star Trek: Discovery, Nurses, Wyonna Earp, and Killjoys.

Robbie is also one of Hanna’s former colleagues, as well as her ex.

Toby Sandeman is a double gold medalist, NBD, for the 200-meter dash. He recently started acting and has had nothing but lead or major supporting roles in the shows The Royals, Power Book III: Raising Kanan, The Game, and Running Point as well as the Netflix movie Voicemails for Isabelle.

Doghouse is yet another one of Hanna’s former colleagues.

Also known as Phillip Brooks, CM Punk is a WWE wrestler. He’s not totally new to professional acting either. Some of his prior credits include Heels, Mayans MC, and Revival as well as movies like Night Patrol and Zootopia 2.

We don’t know what her supporting role is yet, but Jack does have a “PR relationship” with another actress in the book...

Ava Max is a singer-songwriter best known for her acclaimed, very popular song “Sweet But Psycho.” This is her acting debut!

#6838. Inside Prince Harry and Meghan Markle’s Shock Christmas Plans as They’re “Snubbed” From Major Royal Event

The couple has reportedly been snubbed from a family tradition.

It’s been a turbulent year for the Sussexes, as the family relocated back to the UK earlier this year. As with any major life shift, it’s come with lots of adjustment, including not one but two new schools for Archie and Lilibet. Adding into the mix the shocking allegations against Meghan’s father, it’s clear that the family has been weathering a fierce storm.

However, they continue to appear united, with Meghan Markle and Prince Harry continuing to make joint public appearances, including at the European Parliament earlier this month.

Now, as the year winds down, there are questions hanging in the air regarding the family’s Christmas plans amid ongoing alleged tensions between Harry, Meghan, and the wider Royal Family.

Recent reports in the Daily Express claim Harry and Meghan have been left off the guest list for Princess Catherine’s annual Christmas carol concert, which is due to take place on December 11 with a theme of “Together at Christmas.”

This comes amid various insiders claiming that Harry and Meghan weren’t invited to another festive royal tradition: the annual Sandringham Christmas, which sees the royals congregate at their Norfolk estate and attend a church service.

Speaking to The Sunday Times, a source said, “They know it would be too awkward and too difficult [to spend Christmas with the royal family]. They also know they’re not going to be invited, and they’re not waiting for an invitation.”

Amid this tension, People has reported that the family will be spending the holidays with Meghan’s mother, Doria Ragland, in California, avoiding a UK-based festive season.

#6837. Tom Holland and Zendaya Wore a Couple of Fantastic Gold Watches to the Governors Awards

The timepieces themselves? Drastically different. The vibes? Entirely in sync.

Tom Holland and Zendaya were among the stars attending this past weekend’s Governors Awards in Los Angeles, where both made interesting choices when it came to their watches.

Holland, dressed in a black double-breasted Louis Vuitton dinner suit, white shirt, and black bow tie—with tan leather Christian Louboutin boots—was photographed pulling back his cuff to reveal a rose-gold Tank Louis Cartier on a leather strap.

Introduced in 1922, it's one of watchmaking's great enduring designs, with a list of famous admirers that includes Andy Warhol, Yves Saint Laurent, and Princess Diana.

Zendaya, meanwhile, went vintage. Her choice was a small, chic, solid-gold Rolex dating from the 1960s, believed to be the very watch she bought at a vintage market in her hometown of Oakland just over a week ago.

According to the San Francisco Chronicle, the actress spotted the watch at a stall run by independent dealer Wrist Glow Co., tried it on, went off to browse and returned an hour later to buy it. The price? A little over $6,000.

Watch-spotters have identified the Rolex as a ladies' cocktail watch, reference 8196, although that identification has yet to be independently confirmed.

So, what have we learned? Perhaps that for two stars who do old-school Hollywood glamour so well, there are few better choices for an awards-night watch than a classic in gold. Which one you go for, though, remains a matter of personal taste.
